# Source-unread LLM playtest B — 2026-10-20

Only the browser CLI and its rendered text/screenshots were used. No source, design, tests, or other playtest records were read.

## Verbatim command and output log

```text
$ node scripts/browser-drive.ts 4793 look
A FORTNIGHT IN THE MAHALLE

Hearsay
Someone will hear.
☕

You are Hatice. You keep the tea garden.
Your son hopes to marry the baker’s daughter.
This morning, you overhear something true.

Start

Fourteen days. Six ways to answer.
Your words will find their way home.

Open a notebook
[buttons] 1. Start | 2. Open a notebook
[screenshot] scratch/playtest-b/001.png
```

THE END OF THE FORTNIGHT

Words find their way.

Rıza has agreed. Emre and Elif may marry.

Your garden ended lively. No neighbour confronted you at your gate.

A FORTNIGHT IN THE MAHALLE

Hearsay
Someone will hear.
☕

You are Hatice. You keep the tea garden.
Your son hopes to marry the baker’s daughter.
This morning, you overhear something true.

ResumeStart another fortnight

Fourteen days. Six ways to answer.
Your words will find their way home.

$ node scripts/browser-drive.ts 4793 tap Resume
THE END OF THE FORTNIGHT

Words find their way.

Rıza has agreed. Emre and Elif may marry.

What changed

Your garden ended lively. No neighbour confronted you at your gate.

One question to carry

Whose trust mattered when you chose what to say?

The paths your words took

Now you can see the whole route, including conversations you did not witness.

## Visual inspection

Tool call: `functions.view_image({"path":"/Users/abdul/code/hearsay/scratch/playtest-b/025.png"})`.
Output: the screenshot image at that path. The map labels overlap the people and some buildings near the fountain and garden. Main action controls are readable and well separated. The long table guest list truncates visibly with an ellipsis. The feed begins below the action controls.

## Candid observations after completing the fortnight

I used no source, design notes, tests, or other playtest records. All browser CLI output above is complete and unedited, including the long ending route. Tool console previews were shortened only after saving full outputs to this file. I did not click Send or enable anonymous events.

- I reached the ending. Rıza agreed to the marriage, the garden ended lively, and nobody confronted Hatice. This followed repeated lies about her son, an opposite claim about him, a retraction, a knowingly false kind claim about Kemal, and a knowingly false cruel claim directly to Rıza. I cannot infer the simulation rules, but the outcome did not make those provocations feel costly. I expected some personal consequence or an explanation of why none occurred.
- All six verbs were exercised successfully on Day 1. Repeated Tell produced two separate identical claims. Ask then elicited Fatma saying Emre seemed dishonest. This was a satisfying early signal that the lie affected a listener.
- Defend unexpectedly changed the selected dishonest claim to honest. Both claims counted as lies. Without an explicit preview of what Defend will say, I could easily make a statement I did not intend. The belief warning next to the selector does not describe the opposite assertion that Defend actually makes.
- Make amends clearly said the claim was withdrawn and Fatma would reconsider. However, the later ending still showed Fatma spreading the dishonest claim through Safiye, Nermin, Selma, and Selin. That is potentially a strong consequence: correction does not recall a rumour. The ending did not explain whether these were old copies, incomplete correction, or continued repetition.
- The strongest moment was the visible 12:45 action where Kemal helped Safiye. After that I selected cruel and the game called it a lie. This gave evidence a concrete role, although it was not obvious why hearing many accusations had not settled Hatice's belief beforehand.
- The opening promises overhearing something true, but the first feed entry only concerns the unanswered marriage proposal. Many early neighbours repeat that Kemal is cruel. I could not identify a specific initial witnessed incident establishing the promised truth.
- Fatma's moral objections gave her a distinct voice. Several other neighbours differed mainly in their opening phrase, then repeated exactly the same Kemal claim. Repeated statements took up most of the feed. I wanted fewer repetitions and clearer new information.
- Gallop made the fortnight practical to finish. It slowed for the noon question. After answering that question, several wait30 commands skipped multiple days. I saw few new decisions during that stretch. This test therefore verifies the ending and broad progression, not attentive play through every later-day event.
- Sometimes an action shown as enabled in the preceding output returned 'That button is greyed out.' The village was moving quickly; I cannot distinguish a timing artifact of the browser harness from a normal interaction race.
- Notebook download was clickable at noon and at the ending. There was no visible confirmation. I did not inspect downloads or test import, so file creation and round-trip validity remain unverified.
- Reload returned to the title with Resume. Resume returned to the same ending and route. Persistence worked for this completed run.
- The ending route is useful evidence, but thousands of lines of repeated transmissions overwhelm the story. I would want the specific routes started by Hatice and their consequences first, with the full village route available separately.
- The map provides atmosphere. In the inspected screenshot its central labels overlap people and buildings, so it is less useful for locating someone. The main controls are legible.

My verdict: I understood the premise and could operate all six actions. The initial lie and later visible reversal created interest. I lost interest when the village mostly repeated traits and the ending gave me the desired marriage without explaining how my reckless conduct mattered. The game needs stronger readable consequences and a concise account of which choices changed the outcome. This is a player reaction, not a claim about hidden implementation.
